71:T20fa,# Hosted-agent platform hardening: assessment + workplan

Owner directive: make the hosted-agent container system (Hetzner cloud) battle-tested — find bugs, merge/fix what's open, get deep regression + full e2e coverage into CI, and design for backup, user deactivation, and accidental-container-death recovery. **Explicitly deferred to a later phase: idle-detection auto-freeze / wake-on-request cost optimization.**

This epic is the synthesis of a four-way survey (2026-07-09): full architecture read of the control plane, 3 weeks of closed-issue incident history, a CI/test-coverage audit, and a login/onboarding flow map. File refs verified against `origin/develop`.

## What we HAVE (verified, more than the incident history suggests)

**Lifecycle state machine** (`packages/cloud/shared/src/db/schemas/agent-sandboxes.ts`): `pending → provisioning → running`, plus `stopped` (suspend: container removed, slot freed, DB kept), `sleeping` (cold storage: durable backup then teardown, zero compute), `disconnected`, `error`, `deletion_pending/failed`. Driven by a jobs queue with 3 retries + stale-job recovery, executed by the `provisioning-worker` daemon (`packages/scripts/cloud/admin/daemons/provisioning-worker.ts`), which is the live orchestrator (the `container-control-plane` sidecar is effectively deprecated).

**Crash recovery, today**: containers run with `--restart unless-stopped` + Docker HEALTHCHECK (`docker-sandbox-provider.ts:1254-1263`); heartbeat loop probes `/api/health` over headscale with hysteresis → `disconnected` (`eliza-sandbox.ts:4218-4287`) → `dedicated-always` agents get re-probed and idempotently re-provisioned (`provisioning-jobs.ts:2939-2972`); stuck-`provisioning` rows are reconciled (`:3004`); nodes get SSH health checks with auto-disable after N consecutive failures + autoscaler replacement (`docker-node-manager.ts:317-428`); the daemon has a watchdog self-restart under systemd + an external Redis-heartbeat monitor that alerts when the daemon itself dies.

**Backups, today**: `agent_sandbox_backups` with full/incremental, manifest covering DB (pglite-files or postgres-rows) + media + vault + character + state-dir, KMS-AES-256-GCM encryption, 6-hourly auto-backup cron + retention pruning, `pre-shutdown`/`pre-upgrade` snapshots, restore-on-wake, and a daemon boot-refusal on ephemeral `memory` KMS (the countermeasure to the incident below).

**Deactivation, today**: suspend/resume and sleep/wake job types + API routes exist (`v1/eliza/agents/[agentId]/{suspend,resume,sleep,wake}`); billing-driven auto-suspend on credit exhaustion with 48h warning exists. What does NOT exist is any user-facing product surface for it.

**CI, today**: mock-lane coverage is broad (~130 cloud-api PGlite unit tests, 16 server-backed e2e groups, full-journey Playwright suite over a mock stack incl. provision/deprovision/sleep-wake/suspend-resume/scheduled-backup); real-infra coverage is `hetzner-e2e.yml` nightly (provision → deploy trivial agent via staging API → `status.get` ping → teardown) + a 30-min leak reaper + `hetzner-sleep-wake-smoke.yml` (provision A → backup → **destroy A** → provision B → restore → verify) which is **dispatch-only**.

## Incident patterns (3 weeks of closed issues)

1. Provisioning double/orphan races (#15405, #15203, #15423/#15477, #15419, #15429, #9071/#10919, #10854) — the largest cluster.
2. Hand-applied env drift on CP boxes silently killing provisioning (#15160 DB split, #15347 VPN-injection flag, #15135, #14576) — countermeasure emerged: deploy-workflow env reconcile + the #15401 drift ledger.
3. CI runners cohabiting agent robots destroying hosts (#15398, #14628, #14188, #15503, #12986, #11108, #10353, #13617) — placement policy call still unowned.
4. Blue/green upgrade state-machine mismatches (#15173, #15357, #15101, #15384, #15132).
5. PGlite pid-1 stale-lock crash-loop regressed once already (#11222 → #15228).
6. Hardening-introduces-outage (#12468 → #12587).
7. Tailnet fragility (#15261 → #15347).
8. Rotting credentials (#13960, #14424).
9. **Backups were silently undecryptable for weeks** (staging ran `ELIZA_KMS_BACKEND=memory`; every pre-upgrade snapshot failed `key not found`, old backups permanently lost — surfaced in #15310). Boot-refusal now exists, but nothing continuously proves backups are restorable.

## GAPS → workplan

Deferred per owner: idle auto-sleep + wake-on-request (agent-router deliberately does not wake `sleeping/stopped` agents — `agent-router.ts:133-141`).

### A. CI false-green + real-infra cadence (in flight)
1. `test:cloud` never runs `packages/cloud/services/**`; container-control-plane + operator have no `test` script → their tests run in NO lane (`packages/scripts/test-cloud-run.mjs:98-104`). Fix: wire services into the lane + add `test` scripts.
2. `hetzner-sleep-wake-smoke.yml` (the real crash/restore drill) is dispatch-only → schedule it nightly with the same secret-gated honest-skip as `hetzner-e2e.yml`.
3. Nightly `hetzner-e2e` proves only a `status.get` ping → extend to a real chat turn (`message.send` + reply assertion) so the nightly is provision → **chat** → teardown.
4. `monetized-loop-nightly.yml` real path is a green-but-skipped scaffold (needs staging credentials — needs-human).

### B. Backup verification (in flight)
5. Continuous backup-restorability verification: scheduled daemon cycle that samples recent backups, verifies KMS-decryptability + manifest content hashes (without full restore), alerts through the existing health-monitor path. Direct countermeasure to incident 9.
6. Restore integrity gate on wake: validate backup hashes before discarding old compute identity (`eliza-sandbox.ts:472-479` currently degrades to fresh boot on unrecoverable statuses).
7. (Design) Off-box protection for local-PGlite/host-volume state: today backup = live `/api/snapshot` pull from a running agent; a node that dies with no prior snapshot loses volume state. Options: tighter auto-backup interval for local-state agents, node-level volume snapshots, or restic-style off-box sync.

### C. Lifecycle correctness (in flight)
8. `deletion_failed` rows still count against org agent capacity (flagged in-code at `provisioning-jobs.ts:3080-3082`).
9. Orphan-container reconciler ships OFF by default (`ORPHAN_RECONCILER_ENABLED`) — recommend: arm in staging via the deploy-workflow env reconcile, watch a week, then arm in prod (owner sign-off — it force-removes containers).
10. Disconnected `dedicated-lazy` agents stay dark until manual resume (no auto-rebuild by design + no wake-on-request) — at minimum surface the state to the user instead of silent 404s.

### D. User-facing deactivation (in flight)
11. Product surface for suspend/sleep/wake ("deactivate my agent, stop the burn, keep my data") wiring the existing APIs, with billing transparency copy; adjacent to the #15355 upgrade-flow surface.

### E. Login/onboarding hardening (in flight)
12. #15600 prod EVM wallet sign-in dead-end (`@metamask/connect-evm` unresolved in the `@wagmi/connectors` chunk) + click-through e2e so a dead connector can't ship green again.
13. Auth boundary fixes: SIWE/SIWS nonce 500-on-Redis-throw → 503 (broke staging sign-in once; `CLOUD_ONBOARDING_PROVISIONING_REVIEW.md` §8c/§7b), wallet-signup orphan-org window (org+credits committed before user row, EVM+Solana), fire-and-forget `ensureUserHasApiKey` on Workers, `postMessage("*")` in cli-login page, silent dev-login config-persist degrade.

### F. Ops residuals owned by other lanes (not duplicated here)
- #15347 fresh-provision headscale verification + ~47 legacy agent re-provision; #15160 ghost-row sweep (cloud-hardening agent).
- #15398 prod-2 disk cleanup execution (needs node access); runner/robot placement policy (needs-human).
- #15346 prod onboarding-auth QA pass (qa-agent's sign-in matrix lane).
- Blocked-on-human set: #13960, #15218, #13307, #13309, #13117, #13098, #13007, #14628, #13752 et al.

## Definition of done for this epic
- All "in flight" items above landed as PRs with real evidence (tests executing in CI provably, nightly workflows green on real infra, screenshots/e2e for UI).
- The mock full-journey suite + real-infra nightlies together cover: signup → provision → chat → crash → auto-recovery → backup → restore → deactivate → reactivate → delete.
- No silently-skipped "green" lanes for cloud services.72:T86ae,Work item **B7** of epic #15603 ("Off-box protection for local-PGlite/host-volume state"). Design only — read-only research against `develop` @ 2026-07-09; all file:line refs verified. **v2** — revised per the [adversarial critique](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/issues/15783#issuecomment-4929917326); changelog at the bottom.

**Problem.** A dedicated agent's entire persistent state lives on a single Hetzner node's local disk. The only off-box copy is a live `/api/snapshot` HTTP pull from the *running* agent, taken 6-hourly (auto cron) plus at sleep/upgrade — **and only agents whose image actually serves that route get even that** (see loss windows below). If the node's disk dies between snapshots, everything since the last backup is gone; if it dies before the first snapshot ever succeeds, everything is gone. There is no node-level volume snapshot or replication for agent containers.

---

## 1. Current-state map

### Where state lives, per execution tier

Execution tiers: `shared | dedicated-lazy | dedicated-always | custom` (`packages/cloud/shared/src/db/schemas/agent-sandboxes.ts:88`).

| Tier / mode | DB state | Files (media, vault, config, workspace) | On-box exposure |
|---|---|---|---|
| **shared** (default for new agents) | shared cloud Postgres (Railway) | n/a (container-free) | none — off-box by construction |
| **dedicated, `ELIZA_AGENT_LOCAL_STATE=1`** (default for provisioned containers since #8696) | **PGlite at `/root/.eliza/.pgdata`** → host `/data/agents/<agentId>/eliza/.pgdata` | media store `/root/.eliza/media`, vault files, `eliza.json`, state-dir misc, `/app/data` workspace — all on the same host dir | **everything** on one node's local disk |
| **dedicated, `ELIZA_AGENT_LOCAL_STATE=0`** (legacy/opt-out) | shared Postgres via injected `DATABASE_URL` (`database_uri` mode) | same host volume as above | files only; DB off-box |

Key refs:
- Bind mounts: `-v /data/agents/<id>:/app/data` and `-v /data/agents/<id>/eliza:/root/.eliza` — `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/docker-sandbox-provider.ts:1313-1314`; host path from `getVolumePath()` — `docker-sandbox-utils.ts:429-434`.
- Local-state default + `PGLITE_DATA_DIR=/root/.eliza/.pgdata`: `managed-eliza-config.ts:279-287`. Rationale for the default (shared-Postgres "too many clients" #8696 + ~166 ms/query Railway latency dominating dedicated-chat turns) is documented at `managed-eliza-config.ts:222-236`.
- `database_uri` mode selector: `computeManagedAgentDbEnv()` — `eliza-sandbox.ts:452-478`; `database_uri` is the shared Railway `DATABASE_URL` (`provisionAgentDatabase()` — `eliza-sandbox.ts:6183-6204`; column at `schemas/agent-sandboxes.ts:115`).

### The backup pipeline, today

- Agent-side `POST /api/snapshot` (`packages/agent/src/api/server.ts:2013`) → `createAgentSnapshot()` (`packages/agent/src/services/agent-backup.ts:722-790`): a manifest with per-component sha256 covering the DB (**PGlite `dumpDataDir("gzip")` under `runExclusive`** — `agent-backup.ts:626-657` — a *consistent* logical dump, or `postgres-rows` when on shared DB), the content-addressed media store, vault files, character+config, and remaining state-dir files, all base64 in one JSON body.
- Control-plane pull: `fetchSnapshotState()` (`eliza-sandbox.ts:5958-6004`), 120 s timeout (`:353`), **no size cap** — the pull is always the *full* state even though storage may be incremental.
- Storage: `agent_sandbox_backups.state_data` jsonb in the cloud Postgres, KMS-AES-256-GCM field-encrypted under the org DEK (`db/crypto/agent-backups.ts`), full-or-incremental planning (`eliza-sandbox.ts:4161-4215`), retention `MAX_BACKUPS = 10` (`eliza-sandbox.ts:327`) via chain-safe `pruneBackups` (`db/repositories/agent-sandboxes.ts:1346-1370`).
- Cadence: cron `0 */6 * * *` (`packages/cloud/api/wrangler.toml:60`) → `v1/cron/agent-backups/route.ts` (tunable `?intervalMs=&max=`) → `enqueueScheduledBackups()` default `minIntervalMs = 6h`, `maxAgents = 200` per tick (`provisioning-jobs.ts:1476-1530`). Plus `pre-shutdown` at sleep (`executeSleep` — `eliza-sandbox.ts:4955-5060`; sleep **fails closed** if no durable backup can be captured or found) and `pre-upgrade` snapshots.
- **The route is not universally deployed.** The codebase asserts present-tense that "the deployed elizaOS (V2) agent image … does not expose the bridge `/api/snapshot` route — only the cloud-agent template image (and the in-memory test double) do" (`eliza-sandbox.ts:317-324`). Snapshot attempts against such agents return the `SNAPSHOT_ENDPOINT_UNSUPPORTED` sentinel and are treated as a benign skip (`eliza-sandbox.ts:325`, `:4125-4133`).
- The agent can also write **local** KMS-encrypted backup envelopes (`*.agent-backup.json` under the state dir, `createLocalAgentBackup` — `agent-backup.ts:865`) — but those land on the *same* host volume, so they add nothing against node loss today. They are also written with a plain `fs.writeFile` (`agent-backup.ts:875`) — **not** tmp+rename — which matters the moment anything starts sweeping that spool off-box (§3 Phase 3).

### Exact loss windows per failure mode (local-state dedicated agent)

| Failure | What survives | Durability loss | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Container crash** | host volume intact; `--restart unless-stopped` (`docker-sandbox-provider.ts:1295`) + Docker healthcheck (`:1300-1304`) restart it | ≈ 0 (in-flight writes only; PGlite recovers like power loss) | availability risk is the PGlite stale-lock crash-loop (#11222 → #15228), not durability |
| **Node crash / reboot (disk survives)** | volume intact; containers restart on boot | 0 **if the container resumes on the same node**. If the control plane instead re-provisions to another node, restore comes from the last backup → **silent rewind of up to 6 h+** even though the disk was fine | heartbeat → `disconnected` → `dedicated-always` re-provision path (`provisioning-jobs.ts:2939-2972`) makes this rewind reachable in practice |
| **Node disk loss / node death** | only `agent_sandbox_backups` rows in cloud Postgres | **route-bearing agents: everything since the last successful snapshot — up to 6 h + sweep/queue latency in the best case. Route-less agents: unbounded — total loss of all state since provisioning** | this is the B7 gap |

Worst cases that make the window unbounded or the cadence a fiction:

1. **Never-backed-up agents (route-less images).** The sweep only enqueues rows with `bridge_url IS NOT NULL` (`provisioning-jobs.ts:1501-1506`), and images without `POST /api/snapshot` return the `SNAPSHOT_ENDPOINT_UNSUPPORTED` sentinel, treated as a **benign skip** (`eliza-sandbox.ts:4125-4133`). Given `eliza-sandbox.ts:317-324`'s present-tense claim that the deployed V2 agent image lacks the route, **this population may be the majority of the running fleet right now** — for them the honest loss window is not "up to 6 h", it is *everything, always*. Phase 0 measures the population; Phase 1 makes the fleet-image rollout an explicit deliverable (§3).
2. **Persistently failing snapshots.** A large state that can't finish inside the 120 s pull timeout, or a chronically unhealthy bridge, fails every sweep; `last_backup_at` goes stale and nothing pages anyone (epic item B5's verifier covers decryptability of *existing* backups, not staleness).
3. **Sweep starvation — reachable today, at any cadence.** The due-set query is `.where(...).limit(maxAgents)` with **no `ORDER BY`** (`provisioning-jobs.ts:1484-1504`), so which 200 due rows get picked each tick is planner-dependent and nondeterministic. Sentinel skips return `{success:false}` and never bump `last_backup_at` (`eliza-sandbox.ts:4125-4133` — correctly so, bumping would mask staleness), so every route-less agent stays **perpetually due** and permanently competes for the 200-row window. As the perpetually-due population approaches `maxAgents`, specific healthy agents can be starved of backups **indefinitely at the current 6 h cadence** — tightening the interval without fixing this multiplies the wasted budget. Fixing the sweep is an explicit Phase 1 deliverable (§3).

**Honest fleet-wide statement:** "up to 6 h" describes only the route-bearing, non-starved subset of the fleet. Until the Phase 1 image rollout completes and the sweep is fixed, the pre-rollout fleet's realistic loss window ranges from 6 h to *unbounded*, and no alert distinguishes the two.

---

## 2. Options analysis

### (a) Tighten the auto-backup interval for local-state agents (e.g. 15 min)

*Mechanism already exists*: the cron route accepts `?intervalMs=&max=`; a second fan-out on the existing `*/15 * * * *` cron slot (`wrangler.toml:58`) filtered to local-state agents (`environment_vars->>'ELIZA_AGENT_LOCAL_STATE' = '1'`) is a config-plus-small-query change.

- **RPO:** 6 h → ~15 min *for route-bearing agents*; a strict no-op for agents that 404 the endpoint — which is why this option is gated behind the image rollout (§3 Phase 1).
- **Consistency:** the *best* of all options — `dumpDataDir` under `runExclusive` is a coherent logical dump, and the existing manifest/restore path (hash-verified, refuses tampered bytes) is reused unchanged.
- **Cost at fleet scale:** the pull is always FULL state (no incremental fetch), base64-JSON over the bridge. For N local-state agents averaging D MB of state: `96 × N × D` MB/day through the provisioning worker + one KMS encrypt + a jsonb write each. At N=1,000, D=100 MB that is ~9.6 TB/day of pull traffic and ~150 snapshot-jobs/hour-equivalent load (est. 45 s/job, `maxAttempts 2`). `maxAgents=200`/tick caps a 15-min sweep at ~200 agents per tick — beyond that the fleet silently falls behind its own interval (and without the sweep ordering fix, *which* agents fall behind is nondeterministic).
- **Agent-side cost:** `runExclusive` blocks DB writes for the dump duration → a latency spike on the live chat path every 15 min; grows with DB size.
- **Retention caveat:** `MAX_BACKUPS=10` at 15-min cadence shrinks the retained history from ~2.5 days to **2.5 hours** unless `MAX_BACKUPS` is raised (which multiplies Postgres storage; incremental rows soften but don't remove this).
- **Blast radius:** no new infrastructure; risk concentrates in worker throughput and shared-Postgres storage/IO. Failure mode is visible (job failures), not silent.

### (b) Node-side incremental sync of the host volume to R2 (restic/rclone-style)

A per-node systemd timer/sidecar shipping `/data/agents/*` to R2 (S3-compatible; the Worker already has a `BLOB` R2 binding). Content-addressed dedup makes network cost ≈ churn *for the plain-file set* (see the spool caveat under §3 Phase 3 — dedup is **zero** for encrypted envelopes); 5–15 min cadence is cheap.

- **Consistency caveat — the honest one:** copying a **live PGlite data dir** file-by-file yields a torn `$PGDATA` (equivalent to copying a running Postgres without a backup barrier); the restore may be corrupt or refuse to start, and there is no `pg_start_backup` equivalent in PGlite. So a naive restic of the whole volume gives **crash-consistent-at-best, torn-at-worst** DB copies. The *rest* of the volume is fine: the media store is content-addressed (sha256 filenames, immutable), vault/config files are small atomic writes.
- **Workable shape:** exclude `.pgdata`, ship media + vault + config + workspace + the agent's **local backup envelopes** (`*.agent-backup.json` spool — already KMS-encrypted, hash-manifested, consistent because they embed a `dumpDataDir` dump). That turns (b) into a consistent design instead of a corrupt-restore lottery — provided the envelope writes become atomic first (§3 Phase 3).
- **Cost:** R2 ≈ $0.015/GB-mo, zero egress; node CPU for scans; per-agent (or per-node) restic repo keys must come from KMS with **escrow off-box** — a repo key that only lives on the node defeats the purpose. Same for the local-envelope KMS root key (`ELIZA_KMS_BACKEND=local` root-key resolution, `packages/security/src/kms/index.ts:87+`): shipping ciphertext whose only key died with the node is the #15310 incident again in new clothes.
- **Blast radius:** a new daemon on every node and a new *silent-rot* failure mode — it needs the same staleness alerting as (a), plus a periodically exercised restore drill. It also creates a **second backup format** unless restore is unified through the existing manifest path.

### (c) Hetzner Cloud volumes (+ snapshots) for agent containers

Why the app-container path exists but agents don't use it: `hetzner-volumes.ts` was built for **stateful project/app containers** — its only production consumer is coding containers (`coding-containers.ts:45,177`, `use_hetzner_volume: true`; migration `0077_containers_hetzner_volumes.sql`). The file and migration document the constraints that made it a non-starter for the agent fleet:

- **Placement:** Cloud volumes attach only to Cloud-provisioned nodes in the *same location* — "NOT across locations and NOT to auctioned/dedicated boxes" (`hetzner-volumes.ts:15-17`, migration 0077 header). The agent fleet's seed capacity is dedicated/auction Robot boxes (`CONTAINERS_DOCKER_NODES`; the epic's incident cluster 3 is literally about "agent robots"), with Cloud `ccx33` only for autoscaled burst (`containers-env.ts:586`). A durability mechanism that skips the permanent capacity is not a durability mechanism.
- **Density:** Hetzner caps **16 attached volumes per server**. Agent nodes run tens of containers per box; per-agent volumes cannot scale past 16 agents/node.
- **Snapshots don't exist:** the Hetzner Cloud API has **no volume-snapshot endpoint** (server images only — they capture local disks, not volumes; our `hetzner-cloud-api.ts` wraps none because there is none). So option (c) degrades to "network disk that outlives the server": good crash-consistency on node death (ext4 journal + PGlite power-loss recovery), but **no point-in-time history, no protection against volume-level corruption/deletion, no cross-location DR**, ~€0.44+/agent-mo minimum (10 GB floor), plus attach/detach orchestration.
- **Verdict:** fine for the low-count coding-container product it was built for; wrong shape for the agent fleet. Not recommended.

### (d) Steer dedicated agents to managed shared Postgres (`database_uri` mode)

Mechanism exists today: provision with `ELIZA_AGENT_LOCAL_STATE=0` → `computeManagedAgentDbEnv` injects `DATABASE_URL` = shared Railway Postgres. The DB is then **off-box by construction** (provider-side durability/PITR), leaving only media/vault/config/workspace on the volume — exactly the crash-safe file set that (b) syncs without any consistency caveat.

- **Why we left:** #8696 connection exhaustion (since mitigated by `POSTGRES_POOL_MAX=8/MIN=0`, `managed-eliza-config.ts:301-307`) and, decisively, **latency**: ~166 ms/query to Railway × dozens of serial queries per turn was the dominant dedicated-chat latency (`managed-eliza-config.ts:229-236`). Reversing the default re-imports that regression wholesale.
- **What it would take to be viable:** connection pooling at the edge (pgbouncer) + **regional co-location** of the DB with the Hetzner nodes (the latency is geography, not Postgres), or per-agent managed DBs (cost + provisioning surface). Consistency story is the best available (real Postgres, provider PITR).
- **Verdict:** not as a fleet-wide reversal; **yes as an opt-in "durable" tier** for agents whose owners value RPO≈0 for DB state over per-turn latency, and as the standing recommendation for agents that already tolerate shared-DB latency.

### (e) Continuous DB replication: WAL shipping, or row-level write-back (`WriteBackService`)

Two distinct sub-options; v1 conflated them and wrongly claimed no in-repo prior art.

**(e1) Litestream-equivalent WAL shipping — still not viable.** Litestream is SQLite-only. PGlite is an embedded single-connection WASM Postgres: it exposes no `archive_command`, no replication protocol, no `pg_receivewal`, and no external WAL-streaming API; `dumpDataDir` is its only supported consistent export. Building WAL shipping means forking PGlite's WASM VFS layer — out of proportion when the dump pipeline already provides consistent point-in-time exports. Revisit only if upstream PGlite ships a WAL-streaming API.

**(e2) Row-level write-back — prior art exists in-repo and deserves an honest look.** `plugins/plugin-sql/src/write-back/index.ts` ships `WriteBackService` — Electric's Pattern 1 (Online Writes): local PGlite writes are forwarded row-by-row to the cloud API (`{base}/api/v1/eliza/agents/{agentId}/write`, keyed by `ELIZA_CLOUD_WRITE_BASE_URL` / `ELIZA_CLOUD_SERVICE_KEY`), and Electric's `syncShapesToTables` read path (`ELIZA_ELECTRIC_SYNC_URL`) syncs confirmed rows back to PGlite. This is exactly the continuous-replication family — the question is whether hardening it makes continuous DB off-boxing viable without litestream.

As shipped it is **not a durability mechanism**, by its own design:
- fire-and-forget with an **in-memory** queue — queued writes die with the process (`index.ts:9-12`, `:54`);
- **drops writes after `MAX_RETRIES = 5` with a warning log** (`:27`, `:199-221`) — silent divergence, precisely the log-and-continue-fabricates-health failure mode the repo's error doctrine bans on data paths;
- coverage is **per-call-site enqueue**, not adapter-wide interception — any table or write path not routed through it is silently unprotected;
- no transactional boundaries (multi-row atomicity) and no established restore path (reconstructing a coherent PGlite from central Postgres via the Electric read path is not proven for all runtime tables, nor for DDL/migrations).

**Could hardening it change the recommendation?** Honestly: RPO≈seconds for DB state, with **no `runExclusive` dump pause**, beats any cadence option (a) can reach — and it is incremental engineering on an existing seam, not a PGlite fork. The gap list is concrete: a durable on-disk queue (same tmp+rename crash rules as Phase 3's spool), ack'd at-least-once delivery with server-side `writeId` dedup (the `writeId` already exists), **adapter-level interception so coverage is total-or-fail-fast**, DDL/migration handling, and a B5-style verified restore path. That is five real subsystems, and until "coverage is total" is machine-checkable, row-level write-back has the worst durability failure mode available: *looks protected, isn't*.

- **Verdict (changed from v1):** no longer dismissed as "no prior art / not viable" — **rejected from the B7 critical path, kept as the long-term direction.** The dump pipeline is a complete, hash-verified capture→encrypt→store→verify→restore chain that needs cadence/sweep/rollout work; hardened write-back is a partial pipeline needing five new subsystems plus a new verification story, and it covers only DB state (files still need Phase 2 regardless). File it as future epic work — the natural successor to Phase 3 for DB state — and revisit once B5 verification and the Phase 3 spool have landed.

### (f) Node-side atomic filesystem snapshot (LVM-thin/btrfs/ZFS, or `fsfreeze`) + off-box sync

The middle path v1 skipped between "file-by-file copy of live `.pgdata` is torn" and "exclude `.pgdata` entirely": a **point-in-time atomic** snapshot of `/data/agents` at the block/FS layer, then ship the frozen snapshot contents to R2. Atomic snapshot ⇒ **power-loss-consistent**; the container-crash row in §1 already relies on PGlite recovering from power loss, so a `.pgdata` restored from an atomic snapshot starts exactly as if the node had lost power at snapshot time — crash recovery, not a torn copy.

- **Unique property:** zero agent cooperation. This is **the only option on the table that also protects the route-less sentinel population** (worst case 1) — every other consistent-DB option requires the agent image to participate (`/api/snapshot`, spool, or write-back).
- **Honest costs:**
  - Requires `/data` on a snapshot-capable layer. Robot boxes are heterogeneous — typically ext4 on raw disk/md-RAID with no LVM thin pool; converting a live node's storage **in place** is a per-node data-migration/re-provision-scale operation, not a config change.
  - The no-storage-change variant, `fsfreeze` + copy, is only atomic *while frozen*: freezing for the full copy duration stalls writes for **every container on the node** (tens of agents) for minutes; freezing briefly requires a snapshot layer to copy from — which is the storage retrofit again. Realistic (f) therefore means snapshot-capable storage.
  - Restores are **crash-recovery restores**, not clean dumps: the PGlite stale-lock crash-loop (#11222/#15228) is reachable on every restore, and there is no manifest/hash verification unless the shipped snapshot is wrapped in one — otherwise (f) silently becomes a second, weaker backup format.
  - CoW space for deltas, snapshot lifecycle/monitoring on every node, and the same R2 + key-escrow work as (b) — a snapshot sitting on the dying disk is worth nothing.
- **Verdict: adopt conditionally, not as core.** (i) Make snapshot-capable `/data` (btrfs or LVM-thin) the provisioning standard for **new** agent nodes — it is nearly free at provision time and buys a permanent whole-node recovery layer. (ii) If Phase 0 shows the sentinel population is large **and** the Phase 1 image rollout stalls, FS-snapshot+sync on capable nodes is the stopgap that bounds that population's loss window. It is not the primary fix because the image rollout uses an existing, tested, hash-verified pipeline and yields clean-dump restores; (f) yields crash-recovery restores and demands per-node storage surgery on the existing fleet.

---

## 3. Recommendation

**Hybrid of (a) + spool-based (b), keeping the existing hash-verified manifest as the single restore format; (d) as an opt-in tier; (f) as a new-node storage standard + conditional stopgap; reject (c); defer (e2) as the long-term successor for DB state.**

### Phase 0 — measure and alert (no behavior change)
- Fleet metrics: distribution of `last_backup_at` staleness for running local-state agents; count of running local-state agents with **zero** backups, split by cause (`bridge_url IS NULL` vs `SNAPSHOT_ENDPOINT_UNSUPPORTED` sentinel — the latter is the route-less-image population that gates Phase 1); snapshot size p50/p95/max vs the 120 s pull timeout; snapshot job failure rate; per-tick due-set size vs `maxAgents` (starvation pressure).
- **Alert (owner escalation, not a log line)** when any running local-state agent's newest backup age exceeds 2× its target interval, and on any never-backed-up running agent. Fail-fast doctrine: a stale or absent backup is an *error state*, not a warning — extends epic item B5's restorability verifier with staleness.

### Phase 1 — make the existing pipeline actually cover the fleet, then tighten it

Ordered deliverables — the cron change is last because it is a no-op against a fleet the pipeline can't reach:

1. **Fix the sweep (starvation).** Add `ORDER BY last_backup_at ASC NULLS FIRST` to the due-set query (`provisioning-jobs.ts:1484-1504`) so the window always serves the stalest agents first, and stop re-enqueueing known-unsupported images every tick: track `last_backup_attempt_at` separately (bumped on *every* attempt, including sentinel skips, while `last_backup_at` continues to bump only on durable success so staleness alerting stays honest), or equivalently a `snapshot_unsupported` marker cleared on image upgrade. Exit criterion: no running route-bearing agent's `last_backup_at` staleness exceeds 2× its target interval across a full sweep cycle, regardless of the sentinel population size.
2. **Fleet image rollout (closes worst case 1).** Move all running local-state dedicated agents onto an image that serves `POST /api/snapshot`, via the existing upgrade path (pre-upgrade snapshots already gate on a full manifest where available). **Measurable exit criterion: zero `SNAPSHOT_ENDPOINT_UNSUPPORTED` skips across a full sweep cycle, and 100% of running local-state agents hold ≥1 restorable (decrypt + hash-verify, per B5) backup no older than their target interval.** Until this exits, the fleet's real RPO is *not* 6 h and must not be reported as such; if Phase 0 finds the route-less population is the majority, this rollout — not the cron change — **is** the Phase 1 RPO win.
3. **Tighten the interval (RPO 6 h → 15–30 min for the covered fleet).** Fan `agent-backups` on the existing `*/15` cron slot (`wrangler.toml:58`) with `intervalMs` ≈ 15–30 min **scoped to local-state dedicated agents** (env-var filter in `enqueueScheduledBackups`); keep 6 h for shared-DB agents (their volume holds only files). Raise `MAX_BACKUPS` for the tightened cohort so the retention horizon isn't gutted (10 × 15 min = 2.5 h of history); lean on the existing incremental planner for storage. Gate on Phase 0 numbers: per-tick concurrency cap, worker headroom, and measured `runExclusive` dump-pause impact on live chat latency (abort the rollout if p95 turn latency regresses).

### Phase 2 — node-side R2 sync of the crash-safe file set (closes the node-death file gap)
- restic/rclone timer per node shipping `/data/agents/<id>` **excluding the live `.pgdata`** (media store is content-addressed and immutable — ideal for incremental sync; vault/config/workspace are small atomic files) to R2 every 5–15 min.
- Key management is a first-class deliverable: restic repo keys and the agent-local KMS root key must be escrowed off-box (Steward/KMS), never volume-resident-only — otherwise this rebuilds the #15310 undecryptable-backup incident.
- **R2 is backup/restore only, never a serve path.** The content-addressed media store remains the single file store (root `CLAUDE.md` / #8876); nothing may serve bytes from the R2 copy or grow a second storage abstraction around it.
- DB durability continues to come from Phase 1 dumps (consistent by construction); Phase 2 never restores a `.pgdata` copied live.
- New-node storage standard from option (f): provision new agent nodes with snapshot-capable `/data` (btrfs or LVM-thin) so whole-node FS-snapshot recovery is available where it is cheap, and as the stopgap lever if the Phase 1 rollout stalls.

#### Restore composition & skew (binding for the restorer)
Between Phase 1 and Phase 3, DB durability (control-plane dump taken at T1) and file durability (R2 sync taken at T2) come from different clocks; a node-death restore stitched from both is cross-component skewed. Rules:
- **Precedence:** a complete, hash-verified envelope (control-plane backup with full manifest, or a Phase 3 spool envelope) always wins over any stitched restore; among complete envelopes, newest verified wins. A stitched Phase1+Phase2 restore is a fallback only when no complete envelope exists or the newest one predates both parts.
- **Which wins on skew:** in a stitched restore, the DB dump (T1) is authoritative for structured state; the R2 file set (T2) is authoritative for file bytes. Neither is "merged".
- **How the restorer detects skew:** compare T1 vs T2, then verify that every media sha256 referenced from the restored DB resolves in the restored media store — content addressing makes this check exact. DB-referenced-but-missing objects are enumerated; unreferenced extra objects are harmless (the existing GC path owns them).
- **A skewed restore is a *degraded* restore:** surface "DB from T1, files from T2, K missing media objects" to the owner and mark the sandbox restore-degraded — never silently drop, fabricate, or quietly proceed. This extends the no-silent-rewind rule below.

### Phase 3 — write-through spool (structural fix; removes the live-pull single point)
- The agent already writes KMS-encrypted, hash-manifested local backup envelopes (`createLocalAgentBackup`). Schedule that *in the agent* (e.g. every 15 min into a bounded spool dir) and let the Phase 2 node sync ship the spool. The control-plane `/api/snapshot` pull then becomes the *verification* path rather than the only capture path — an agent that is briefly unreachable at sweep time no longer misses its RPO.
- **Atomic spool writes are a deliverable, not an assumption.** `createLocalAgentBackup` today writes the envelope with a plain `fs.writeFile` (`agent-backup.ts:875`). Phase 3 must switch it to tmp+rename: write `<name>.agent-backup.json.tmp` in the same directory, fsync, `rename(2)`, fsync the directory — and the node-side sync must ship only complete files (exclude `*.tmp`). Without this, a sweep can ship a torn *newest* envelope; hash verification makes it fail loudly at restore, but the effective RPO silently widens to the previous envelope.
- **The spool's transfer cost belongs to this recommendation** (not just to option (a)): envelopes are KMS-AES-GCM ciphertext of full state, so the bytes change completely every run and restic/rclone dedup is **zero** for the spool. Naive Phase 3 at 15-min cadence therefore re-imports option (a)'s `96 × N × D` MB/day math as R2 upload *and* doubles it as node-volume writes. Mitigations, in order:
  1. **Incremental envelopes (mandatory deliverable):** reuse the existing full-or-incremental planner shape at the component level — per-component manifests let unchanged components (media, workspace, vault, config — content-addressed or rarely-churning) be *referenced*, not re-shipped; only the DB dump component is inherently full each run.
  2. **Cadence scaling by state size** for large-state agents, with the staleness alerting keeping them honest.
  3. Chunked/**convergent encryption is explicitly rejected**: it leaks ciphertext equality across snapshots and tenants — a confidentiality trade we don't need once (1) exists.
- Restore stays unified: one manifest format, restore **refuses** partial/tampered components (component sha256 verification already does this — keep it), and any restore that isn't byte-complete **fails loudly**. On node-death recovery, surface "restored from backup taken at T; potential loss window X" to the owner — never a silent rewind (this also covers the re-provision-while-disk-was-fine rewind case in the table above).

### Cost of the recommended path (charged where it belongs)

| Component | Steady-state transfer | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Phase 1 tightened pulls | `96 × N × D`/day worst-case through the worker | bounded by cohort scoping + `maxAgents`; full-state pulls, no dedup; visible-failure mode |
| Phase 2 file sync (media/vault/config/workspace) | ≈ churn only | content-addressed dedup works here |
| Phase 3 spool, naive | `96 × N × D`/day to R2 **+ the same again as node-volume writes** | encrypted full-state envelopes defeat dedup entirely — **unacceptable; why incremental envelopes are mandatory** |
| Phase 3 spool, with incremental envelopes | ≈ `96 × N × D_db`/day + file churn (D_db = compressed DB dump size) | the honest run-rate of the recommendation |

### What to measure throughout
- **Actual RPO**: age of the newest *restorable* (decrypt + hash-verify) backup, sampled fleet-wide — not `last_backup_at` alone.
- Sentinel-population count (must reach 0 to exit Phase 1.2) and per-tick due-set size vs `maxAgents` (starvation pressure, must stay < 1 after Phase 1.1).
- Snapshot pull duration p95 vs timeout; dump-pause impact on message-turn latency; worker queue depth per tick.
- Storage growth: `agent_sandbox_backups` (Postgres) and R2 (split file-set vs spool), per cohort.
- Restore-drill success rate: extend the `hetzner-sleep-wake-smoke` provision→destroy→restore drill (epic item A2) to also exercise a Phase 2/3 spool restore **and a stitched skewed restore** nightly.

### Did the recommendation shift after revision?

**The core did not; the path to it did.** The shape — tightened dump cadence + spool-shipped node sync unified on the hash-verified manifest, (d) opt-in, (c) rejected — survives because every alternative still fails on its own terms: (b)-naive and (f) produce crash-recovery-at-best DB copies where the dump path produces clean verified dumps; (c) cannot reach the permanent Robot capacity; (e2), even hardened, needs five new subsystems and total-coverage proof where the dump pipeline needs cadence/sweep/rollout work on a chain that already exists end-to-end. What shifted: **Phase 1 is no longer "a cron change"** — the sweep fix and the fleet-image rollout now precede and gate the interval tightening, because against the pre-rollout fleet the cron change alone protects nobody who isn't already protected; **(f) enters** as a new-node storage standard and conditional stopgap for the sentinel population; **(e2)'s rejection is narrowed** from "not viable / no prior art" to "not the B7 critical path", with `WriteBackService` named as the long-term DB-state successor; and **Phase 3 now owns its true transfer cost**, with atomic spool writes and incremental envelopes as explicit deliverables rather than assumptions.

## 4. Explicitly out of scope
- **Idle-freeze / auto-sleep cost optimization** — deferred by owner directive (see epic #15603 header).
- Wake-on-request routing, backup-restorability verification internals (B5), and restore-integrity-gate-on-wake (B6) — tracked separately in the epic.
- Hardening `WriteBackService` into a durability mechanism (e2) — future epic work, not B7.

77:T3071,## What

The context-retrieval pass + corpus-wide pseudonym consistency stage of the corpus PII scrub pipeline (stage position: between candidate-mining and the LLM-pass), built entirely on the landed rails — `PiiScrubService` (#14808 slice 1, PR #15139) and the `PII_SCRUB` model seam (#14809, PR #14980) — with **zero changes to either**. No new scheduler, no new queue, no second knowledge-graph store.

Closes #14805

### `packages/core/src/security/pii-pseudonym-map.ts` — one person = one replacement everywhere
- `CorpusPseudonymMap`: cluster-keyed (`entity:<entityId>` from the EntityStore alias backbone — **never surface strings**), extending the session pseudonymizer's deterministic minting (`mintSurrogate`, now exported) from per-session to corpus-persistent: mint seed is `(salt, kind, clusterId, attempt)`, salt persisted inside the (secret) snapshot, so the same person deterministically keeps the same pseudonym across runs, artifact types, and assignment orders.
- **Collision behavior:** two distinct people sharing a name are two clusters with two pseudonyms; an alias claimed by 2+ clusters is **ambiguous** — never blind-substituted (a longest-first self-mapping shields it from partial rewrites by contained shorter aliases), surfaced for model escalation with ALL candidate assignments. A newly learned real alias equal to an existing pseudonym re-mints that cluster with a `supersededPseudonyms` audit trail. Re-homing a platform identity to a second cluster **throws** — identity merges stay in the merge engine.
- **Idempotent upsert** (no duplicate clusters/aliases/identities on re-assign) and **pseudonym stability across ruleset bumps** — a `v<ruleset>` bump re-scrubs content (content-hash markers re-key) with the SAME pseudonyms, so a re-scrub never re-links identities.

### `packages/core/src/security/pii-pseudonym-map-store.ts` — the map is a secret artifact
- The alias→pseudonym map inverts the scrub, so it lives **outside the retrievable corpus**: one dedicated runtime-cache key (`pii:pseudonym-map:v1`, next to the scrub done-markers), never a document/memory row, no ingestion path — unreachable via `searchDocuments` / `searchMessages` / `searchMemories` / `SEARCH_KNOWLEDGE`, never chunked or embedded. Confidentiality is structural, plus at-rest AES-256-GCM (v2 settings-secret scheme, dedicated AAD `elizaos:pii-pseudonym-map:v1` for domain separation, `SECRET_SALT` lifecycle).
- **Fail-closed everywhere:** refuses to persist plaintext; wrong key, tampered ciphertext, non-ciphertext value, or a structurally invalid snapshot all **throw** (deliberately stricter than `decryptStringValue`'s return-original-on-error) — a partial map would silently re-mint pseudonyms for already-mapped people, which is worse than stopping the pipeline.

### `packages/core/src/security/pii-context-pack.ts` — the context-retrieval pass
- `assembleContextPack`: per chunk, resolves candidates through a **structural `EntityStore.resolve` seam** (`PiiEntityResolverStore` matches `packages/agent/src/services/knowledge-graph/entity-store.ts` field-for-field; `entityResolverFromStore(kg.getEntityStore())` wires it with zero adaptation — core does not import agent), gathers related fragments (`DocumentService.searchDocuments`, `runtime.searchMemories` under the embeddings throw-never-fabricate doctrine, `adapter.searchMessages` with explicit roomIds), clusters confident resolutions into the map, and emits the bounded pack + the per-chunk `{entityClusterId, surrogate, kind}` assignment slice — **never the whole map, never a real alias**.
- Failure doctrine: absent sources are structurally skipped and audited in `sourcesQueried`; a wired source that throws **propagates** (rails retry the item) — degraded context silently producing a wrong verdict is the failure mode this pass exists to prevent.
- `buildScrubRequestDraft` folds a chunk + pack into the landed `PII_SCRUB_REQUESTED` payload — the exported interface siblings (#14806 transcript anchors, #14807 audio redaction span labels) consume for corpus-consistent replacements.
- `pii-pseudonymizer.ts`: additive `export` on `mintSurrogate` + `compileReplacer` only; no behavior change (fuzz + unit suites green).

## Tests (all in this PR, 57 new; 113 green across the PII surface)

- `pii-pseudonym-map.test.ts` (25): deterministic stability across runs/orders/snapshot-reload; the **hard-gate** at map level (full name in a document, first name in a second document, nickname in chat, @handle in a transcript mirror → one pseudonym; similar-named second person → different one; zero alias survivals; idempotent re-substitution); ambiguity/collision/re-mint/identity-conflict; ruleset-bump × marker-key interplay; blocklist, regex-metachar + unicode aliases, word boundaries, empty/adversarial input; fail-closed snapshot validation.
- `pii-pseudonym-map-store.test.ts` (10): round-trip; ciphertext-at-rest (no alias/pseudonym/clusterId/JSON leak, single dedicated key); wrong-salt/tamper/plaintext/malformed → throw; failed write surfaces.
- `pii-context-pack.test.ts` (18): clustering thresholds; per-chunk slice never leaks unrelated clusters; ranking/bounding; absent-source audit; empty candidates → zero source calls; source throw propagates; `entityResolverFromStore` normalization (identity-based resolution, canonical kinds, candidate cap); `sourcesFromRuntime` wiring (embedding-model gate, explicit-roomIds gate, measured scores only).
- `pii-pseudonym-consistency.test.ts` (4): **end-to-end through the landed rails** — context pack → `buildScrubRequestDraft` → real `PiiScrubService` drain → `scrubWithEscalation` → PII_SCRUB verdicts. One pseudonym per person across all four artifact types; **tier-0 deterministic path: zero model calls**; **fail-closed: residue with no handler throws, retries exhaust into `PII_SCRUB_FAILED`, done-marker never written** (throw-never-fabricate); re-run under same ruleset is a marker no-op, ruleset bump re-scrubs with stable pseudonyms.

Fixes all five actionable findings from the post-merge adversarial review of the backup restorability verifier:

## 1. Missing R2 payload: stamped + alerted, and it can no longer wedge the sampler
- Daemon-path `getObjectText` throws AWS `NoSuchKey` (`object-store.ts:147-159`); the verifier now classifies the S3/R2 object-missing error family (`NoSuchKey` / `NotFound` / `$metadata.httpStatusCode === 404`) as the stamped **`payload-missing`** failure — it alerts like every other failure instead of falling into the unstamped infra-error catch.
- **Errored-row semantics (chosen: stamp the attempt).** Verifier infra errors now stamp `verification_status='errored'` + `verified_at=<attempt time>` (the schema already documents `verified_at` as the last verification *ATTEMPT*). The row falls out of the nulls-first batch head until the re-verify interval elapses — structural starvation-proofing rather than an ad-hoc skip list, with the same retry cadence as everything else. `errored` is distinct from `failed`: a healthy backup is never marked broken by verifier-side breakage (J7 preserved).
- **Persistent-error alert.** A consecutive-attempt streak is persisted in the `infra-error[N]: …` prefix of `verification_error` (survives daemon restarts without a new column) and raises a dedicated alert (`agent-backup-verification-errored` dedup key) at `BACKUP_VERIFICATION_ERRORED_ALERT_STREAK` (default 3).

## 2. Bounded memory on incremental verification
- `getReconstructedBackupState` **is shared** — `eliza-sandbox.ts` uses it for provision auto-restore, `restore()`, wake, and downgrade — so it is **untouched**. The verifier gets its own `reconstructIncrementalStateSequential`: resolves the chain from **metadata only** (`listBackupMetadata`, no payload hydration), then fetches + decrypts **only chain members, one at a time**, releasing each before the next loads. Non-chain backups are never downloaded (previously: parallel download+decrypt of up to 1000 retained backups per sampled row).
- **Per-cycle decrypt budget** `BACKUP_VERIFICATION_MAX_DECRYPT_BYTES` (default 256 MiB), charged with stored-payload bytes *before* decrypting. Budget exhaustion mid-cycle defers the rest of the batch (unstamped — fresh budget re-samples them first next cycle); a single payload larger than the entire budget is a **bounded skip** stamped `errored` (it can never verify under that config; the streak alert surfaces the misfit).

## 3. Steward KMS 404 → `key-unavailable`
`KmsError` now carries the HTTP status (`packages/security/src/kms/types.ts`), populated by the steward adapter; `classifyCryptoError` maps KmsError-404 (structured status, with a message-format fallback) to `key-unavailable`. The #15310 signature no longer depends on the memory backend's `KeyNotFoundError` shape.

## 4. Systemic escalation floor
The fleet-wide KMS-misconfig page requires `BACKUP_VERIFICATION_MIN_SYSTEMIC_SAMPLE` (default 5) sampled rows AND the failure-rate threshold. Per-row failure alerts are unaffected.

## 5. Composite index `(sandbox_record_id, created_at DESC)`
Landed on develop as migration `0174_agent_backup_latest_index` (`agent_sandbox_backups_sandbox_latest_idx`) while this branch was in flight; this PR completes the hand-authored 0169+ pattern by mirroring the index into `ensure-agent-sandbox-schema.ts`. Verified: no other index shares the name; migration applies on fresh PGlite (below).

## Tests (real pipeline, storage boundary observable)
Extended `agent-backup-verifier.test.ts` (17 → 28 tests) on real PGlite + real memory KMS. The R2 cases drive the **real S3 client and the real offload/hydrate/decrypt code against a local HTTP object-store stub** (S3 GET/PUT + `NoSuchKey` XML the AWS SDK deserializes for real) — only the remote storage *server* is simulated, with per-key GET counters:
- `NoSuchKey` → `payload-missing` stamped + alerted
- errored row stamped with attempt → next cycle samples the healthy agent (no head-of-line block)
- errored streak `infra-error[2]` → dedicated alert with backupId + streak
- steward 404 → `key-unavailable`, proven against the **real `StewardKmsAdapter`** (body deliberately avoids "key not found" so the 404 path is what's tested)
- 1-of-1 failure → per-row alert fires, systemic does NOT (floor)
- incremental verification downloads **only** chain members, exactly once each; the sandbox's other retained backups: zero GETs
- oversize payload → bounded skip stamped `errored`, no crash, not re-sampled inside the interval
- budget exhaustion → remainder deferred unstamped, verified next cycle

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)79:T28a7,## Summary

Work item **B6** of #15603: a **restore-integrity gate on hosted-agent wake**.

A sleeping agent's compute identity is already discarded — the stored backup **is** its entire durable state. `executeWake` relied on `provision()`'s built-in latest-backup restore, which degrades unrecoverable snapshots (undecryptable ciphertext, rotated-away KMS key, gone payload) to a **fresh boot**. Right call for a running agent losing volatile session state; for a wake it silently brought the user's agent back **empty**.

### What changed

- **New gate** (`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/wake-restore-integrity.ts`): `executeWake` validates the exact backup the restore will apply **before any compute side effect**, reusing the B5 verification primitives (`verifyBackupRestorability`: real KMS decrypt, sequential chain replay for incrementals, `content_hash` + manifest hashes) — no duplicated verification logic.
  - `verification_status='verified'` within a freshness window (`WAKE_RESTORE_VERIFIED_FRESHNESS_HOURS`, **default 24h**, matching the B5 re-verify cadence) → wakes on the stamp, no re-decrypt.
  - `verification_status='failed'` → hard-fails immediately, no re-decrypt.
  - Anything else → verified for real and stamped with the same field semantics as the verifier cycle.
- **On failure**: the wake **job** fails with a typed `WakeRestoreIntegrityError` naming the backup, its snapshot type/created-at, and the failure kind; the structured failure also lands in the job `result` for pollers. The sandbox **stays `sleeping`** (the gate runs before `provision()`, and `AGENT_WAKE` has no permanent-failure writeback — nothing is deleted, nothing boots fresh). The failure alerts through `sendProvisioningWorkerAlert` (dedup key `agent-wake-restore-integrity`) — the same ops channel the B5 verifier uses. The retention set is scanned (bounded by `WAKE_RESTORE_ALTERNATIVE_SCAN_LIMIT`, budget-capped decrypts) for an **older backup that validates**, which is named in the error.
- **Explicit escape hatches on the wake route** (`packages/cloud/api/v1/eliza/agents/[agentId]/wake/route.ts`), never defaults, mutually exclusive (400 if both):
  - `restoreBackupId` — wake from a validated older backup. Route pre-validates ownership by **metadata only** (cross-agent ids are indistinguishable from missing ones — no existence oracle; mirrors the `/restore` route) and 409s early on a `failed`-stamped backup. The daemon re-enforces ownership, and `provision()` gets a new `restoreOverride` (`{ kind: "from-backup", backupId }`) that **never** degrades an explicit restore to a fresh boot.
  - `forceFreshBoot` — the user accepts data loss; `provision()` runs with `{ kind: "fresh-boot" }`, skipping the restore entirely (the backup chain stays intact, un-degraded and un-pruned).
- **Ops kill switch**: `WAKE_RESTORE_INTEGRITY_ENABLED=0` reverts wake to the ungated legacy behavior (loudly logged). Documented in `packages/cloud/shared/.env.example`.

### Shared-consumer audit (scope requirement 4)

`provision()` is shared by: the provision/resume/restart API routes (v1 + compat), agent-create, `eliza-managed-launch`, the provisioning-jobs `AGENT_PROVISION` handler, the heartbeat disconnect self-heal, `executeResume`/`executeRestart`, the warm-pool creator, and `restore()` (stopped-agent path). **All of them call `provision(agentId, orgId)` with no override and keep the historical latest-backup auto-restore + unrecoverable-snapshot degrade, byte-identical.** Only `executeWake` passes the new optional `restoreOverride`. `degradeUnrecoverableSnapshot` / `isUnrecoverableSnapshotError` / `isPermanentlyLostSnapshot` are untouched. `restore()`'s "stopped agents can only restore the latest backup" constraint is unchanged (out of B6 scope; it could adopt the override later).

Design decisions:
- **Freshness window default = 24h** (`WAKE_RESTORE_VERIFIED_FRESHNESS_HOURS`), aligned with `BACKUP_VERIFICATION_REVERIFY_HOURS` so a fleet covered by the B5 continuous verifier normally wakes on the stamp alone; env-tunable per the requirement.
- **Verifier-infra errors and budget-exceeded payloads THROW** from the gate → the wake job retries; infra breakage is never misread as a bad backup and never becomes a fresh boot.
- Wake-gate stamps use the same row fields/format as the B5 cycle (`verified_at` = attempt time, `verification_error` = `"<kind>: <message>"`), so gate stamps and cycle stamps are indistinguishable to readers, and a failed wake's stamp short-circuits the retry attempts cheaply.

Closes item B6 of #15603.

- `restoreBackupId`+`forceFreshBoot` together → rejected before any side effect
- no backups at all → wake proceeds fresh (nothing durable to discard)
- config defaults / env overrides / kill switch

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
7a:T25cb,Work item **B5** of epic #15603.

## Why

#15310: staging ran `ELIZA_KMS_BACKEND=memory` for weeks. Every 6-hourly backup written to `agent_sandbox_backups` was encrypted under keys that evaporated on the next worker restart — **every backup was permanently undecryptable, and nobody noticed until restores failed with `AEAD decrypt failed`**. A boot-refusal on the ephemeral backend now exists (`assertKmsBackendDurable`, `isEphemeralKmsAllowed`), but that only prevents the *config*; nothing continuously proved that backups **already stored** remain restorable with the *current* KMS keys after rotations, DB repoints, or storage bit-rot.

## What

A scheduled backup-verification cycle in the provisioning-worker daemon:

- **Service** `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/agent-backup-verifier.ts` — `runBackupVerificationCycle()`:
  - **Sampling:** `DISTINCT ON (sandbox_record_id)` newest backup per agent → filter `verified_at IS NULL OR verified_at < now - reverify_interval` → order never-verified first, then longest-stale → bounded batch. Over successive cycles the whole fleet converges to coverage; only the newest backup per agent is sampled because that is what a restore reaches for first (and incremental verification transitively decrypts every ancestor it depends on).
  - **Verification =** resolve payload (inline jsonb or R2 offload) → **decrypt with the real KMS** (proves key availability + AEAD integrity — the check that would have caught #15310) → replay incremental parent chains → validate the row's `content_hash` against the reconstructed state → validate the full-agent manifest's per-file and per-component sha256s over the decrypted artifacts.
  - **What it proves / doesn't:** proves the stored ciphertext decrypts under the current keys, the restore chain reconstructs, and the decrypted artifacts hash-match the manifest. It does **not** boot a container from the state — deliberately no restore into a sandbox, no writes to agent state; read-only against backup storage. The only writes are the verification stamps on the backup row.
  - **Failure classification:** `key-unavailable` (KMS key id/version gone — the #15310 signature), `decrypt-failed` (AEAD auth failure: corrupt ciphertext or wrong key material), `payload-missing`, `invalid-payload`, `chain-broken`, `hash-mismatch`. Verifier-infrastructure errors (DB/object-storage unreachable) are logged loudly but never stamped as backup failures.
- **Schema** (migration `0173_agent_backup_verification.sql` + `ensure-agent-sandbox-schema.ts` idempotent guard): three nullable columns on `agent_sandbox_backups` — `verification_status` (`verified`/`failed`), `verified_at` (last attempt; drives the re-verify interval), `verification_error` (classified failure for triage without a re-run).
- **Alerting:** every failure is a structured `logger.error` + one consolidated cycle alert through the **same ops channels as `provisioning-worker-health-monitor.ts`** (`PROVISIONING_ALERT_SLACK_WEBHOOK` / `PROVISIONING_ALERT_PAGERDUTY_KEY`; `DaemonHealthAlert` gained an optional `dedupKey` so backup incidents don't collapse into the heartbeat incident). When a cycle's failure rate ≥ `BACKUP_VERIFICATION_ESCALATION_PCT` (default 50%), a **separate systemic escalation** fires naming the KMS-misconfig signature.
- **Daemon wiring** (`packages/scripts/cloud/admin/daemons/provisioning-worker.ts`): `processBackupVerificationCycle` as a `runBoundedPhase` in the infra-maintenance sweep (every `WORKER_NODE_HEALTH_INTERVAL`, default 5 min) — off the watchdog critical path, error-isolated like every other phase.
- **Config** (documented in `packages/cloud/shared/.env.example`, **on by default**): `BACKUP_VERIFICATION_ENABLED` (opt-out), `BACKUP_VERIFICATION_BATCH_SIZE` (10), `BACKUP_VERIFICATION_REVERIFY_HOURS` (24), `BACKUP_VERIFICATION_ESCALATION_PCT` (50).

Manifest hashing intentionally mirrors the producer in `packages/agent/src/services/agent-backup.ts` (canonical sorted-key JSON → sha256); cloud-shared cannot import that package, so the module header pins the lockstep requirement. The test fixtures compute producer-side hashes with an *independent* implementation so the verifier is cross-checked, not self-checked.

## Summary

- recover the unsubmitted native-auth fix found on `fix/lp3-native-auth-handoff` during the branch-forensics sweep
- fail closed when Capacitor lacks a native WebAuthn bridge, while preserving passkeys for secure browser/desktop environments with a working platform authenticator

7c:T2b61,Work item **D11** of the hosted-agent platform hardening epic #15603.

## What

The backend fully supports deactivating a hosted dedicated agent without deleting it (`suspend`/`resume`/`sleep`/`wake` job types + API routes), but no product surface exposed it — users could only delete. This ships the user-facing flow, built on the zero-compute `sleep` tier:

- **Agent detail page** (`/dashboard/agents/:id`, `ElizaAgentActions`): a **"Deactivate Agent"** button now sits next to Delete and opens a confirm dialog with plain billing-transparency copy — *"Your agent stops running and stops consuming hourly credits (currently $0.01/hr while running). All of its data is saved in an encrypted backup — nothing is deleted. You can reactivate it anytime. Reactivation restores the backup and can take a few minutes."* The rate shown is the cloud-shared `AGENT_PRICING` constant the hourly billing cron charges from — the client only displays it, no client-side math. Confirming fires the real `POST /sleep` (202 + jobId) through the existing job poller with deactivate-specific progress copy.
- **Deactivated is a designed state, not an error**: a sleeping agent renders a muted info panel ("This agent is deactivated. It is not running and is not consuming hourly credits; its data is kept in an encrypted backup.") with a primary **"Reactivate Agent"** button wired to `POST /wake`, plus an explicit **$0.00/hr** cost cell ("Deactivated — no hourly cost"). Wake re-provisions compute, so the in-between job state shows *"Reactivating — restoring your agent from its backup. This can take a few minutes."* and polls `/api/v1/jobs/:id` exactly like the provisioning UI.
- **Agents table** (`/dashboard/agents`): sleeping rows get the muted `sleeping` badge, an explicit **$0.00/hr** cost badge (tooltip: "Not running — no hourly cost. Data is kept in an encrypted backup."), a **Reactivate** row action, a **Deactivate** row action behind the same confirm dialog, and a **"Deactivated"** status filter. `sleeping` joins the `SandboxStatus` union + terminal poll states (it previously fell through to fallback badge styling with no affordance at all), and a sleeping row's runtime reads "Cloud sandbox" instead of the misleading "Not provisioned".

## Where the surface lives

- `packages/ui/src/cloud/instances/components/agent-actions.tsx` — detail-page Deactivate/Reactivate, confirm dialog, job-progress copy
- `packages/ui/src/cloud/instances/components/eliza-agents-table.tsx` — row actions, confirm dialog, status filter, view-model (`canSleep`/`canWake`)
- `packages/ui/src/cloud/instances/components/agent-cost-badge.tsx` — explicit $0.00/hr for sleeping
- `packages/ui/src/cloud/instances/AgentDetailPage.tsx` — zero-cost cell for sleeping
- `packages/ui/src/cloud/instances/lib/use-sandbox-status-poll.ts` — `sleeping` in the status union + terminal states
- `packages/ui/src/i18n/locales/en.json` — new copy keys

## Scope notes

- **Billing honesty:** the hourly billing service (`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/active-billing.ts`) only charges `running` and `stopped`-with-backup rows — sleeping rows are skipped entirely, so $0.00/hr is the true rate; every displayed figure comes from the isomorphic `@elizaos/cloud-shared` pricing constants.
- **Adjacent state honesty (epic C10):** `stopped`/`disconnected`/`error` rows already had a Resume affordance in both the table (`canStart`) and the detail page (`isStopped`) — left untouched. The silent-404 for disconnected dedicated-lazy agents in the *chat app* is a different component tree and stays with C10.
- Suspend (the light `stopped` tier) keeps its existing button; Deactivate is the flagship zero-compute flow per D11.
- The view-action ratchet does not cover `packages/ui/src/cloud/` (it scans `packages/ui/src/components`); ran it anyway — green. In-app settings `CloudAgentsSection` intentionally not extended (would require a new settings action-twin; the cloud console is D11's surface).

## Tests

- **Unit** (`eliza-agents-table.render.test.tsx`, +3 tests, 9/9 green): `canSleep`/`canWake` derivation incl. shared-tier + busy-row negatives and sleeping-with-cleared-sandbox runtime kind; sleeping row renders the designed state (Reactivate present, Deactivate absent, $0.00/hr badge); the confirm dialog shows the billing copy and Cancel is a real exit.
- **Mock-stack UI e2e** (`packages/test/cloud-e2e/tests/deactivate-reactivate-ui.spec.ts`, 2/2 green): boots the full local stack (PGlite + Hetzner/control-plane mocks + cloud-api Worker + apex frontend) and **clicks the real product**:
  1. *Detail page*: provision → running → open dialog (assert billing copy) → **Cancel path** (nothing fired, still running) → confirm → assert the UI itself fired `POST /sleep` **202** → deactivation progress copy → drive the real DB-backed job pipeline → deactivated panel + `sleeping` badge + $0.00/hr + a durable restore point exists (`GET /backups` ≥ 1) + the bridge honestly refuses traffic ("Sandbox is not running") → click Reactivate → `POST /wake` **202** + reactivation progress copy → job pipeline → running again → the cloud-api DTO shows a re-provisioned container endpoint and that restored endpoint + its conversation/chat surface serve **200** again. (The Worker's own bridge hop routes dedicated traffic via per-agent public subdomains, which don't resolve in the local mock stack — restoration is asserted at the two boundaries the stack genuinely exercises, cloud-api DTO + container endpoint.)
  2. *Agents list*: sleeping row renders distinctly with $0.00/hr → row **Reactivate** fires `POST /wake` 202 → the table converges on `running` without a manual reload.

7d:T18cd,Second slice of #14806 (transcript time anchors): the cloud STT route now returns word/segment timestamps so cloud-transcribed audio can feed the transcript-fragment anchor pipeline (PR #15836) and the PII audio-redaction span math (#14807).

## What changed

- `packages/cloud/api/v1/voice/stt/route.ts` (whisper lane): sends `response_format=verbose_json` + `timestamp_granularities[]=word` + `timestamp_granularities[]=segment` (OpenAI-spec fields), and returns `segments[]` / `words[]` as `{text, startMs, endMs}` next to `transcript`. A server that ignores the fields still returns `text` — the existing DTO contract is unchanged (keys are simply absent).
- Whisper 200-boundary hardening (J3): a 200 whose body is unparseable JSON, a non-object, or missing the required `text` string is translated to a structured 502 — never a healthy-empty transcript.
- New pure parser `whisper-timestamps.ts` (unit-testable without the route's billing/service graph, same pattern as `whisper-model.ts`): seconds→ms conversion, strict per-entry validation (non-empty text, finite non-negative start/end, `end >= start`), malformed entries **dropped and logged, never coerced**; all-malformed or missing arrays yield **absent** DTO keys, never fabricated zeros/empties.
- `route.test.ts` (renamed from `route.whisper.test.ts` — it now covers the whole route): drives the REAL Hono handler end to end with only auth/billing/provider modules mocked at the module boundary. Covers the shared upload-validation gates (non-multipart, missing file, 25MB cap, declared-MIME allowlist, magic-number gate, spoofed-extension rejection), auth-failure mapping, the free whisper lane (timestamps DTO + the malformed-200 matrix), and the billed ElevenLabs lane (billing/reservation wiring, 402 insufficient credits, 429/402/503/500 error mapping with reservation refunds). A final describe, gated on `ELIZA_VOICE_LIVE_RAILWAY=1`, runs the same real route against the deployed Railway faster-whisper with real Kokoro-synthesized speech.
- TS under the cloud-api workers-types tsconfig: the test's `instanceof File` narrowing (TS2358 on the workers-types `FormDataEntryValue`) is replaced with a structural `isFilePart` guard, and bare `res.json()` expect sites are pinned to `unknown` via a `readJson` helper — no `any` casts, no ts-ignore.
- ElevenLabs SDK surface untouched (scribe timestamps are a follow-up if wanted).

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)7e:T140d,## What / why

Closes **cluster 1 of #15759**. The per-plugin keyless-e2e coverage gate (#8801,
`packages/scripts/e2e-coverage/check-e2e-coverage.test.ts`) fails in the Zero-Key
scenario-runner lane because `plugin-birdclaw`, `plugin-elizacloud`, and
`plugin-meetings` each expose an agent action surface with **no** `pr-deterministic`
scenario naming them. The baseline is a shrink-only ratchet whose `$comment`
forbids adding entries to silence it — so the required fix is **real scenarios**,
not baseline edits.

This adds one keyless (`lane: "pr-deterministic"`) scenario per plugin. Each drives
a real action handler (validate + handler firing through the real runtime) under
the deterministic LLM proxy (`SCENARIO_USE_LLM_PROXY`) with **zero credentials** —
no live model, no network, no browser — and each carries an effect-proving
`custom` final check (not an echo assertion).

## What each scenario exercises

| Scenario | Action | Real path exercised | Effect proof |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `birdclaw.search-archive` | `BIRDCLAW` (`search`) | Writes a fake `birdclaw` CLI to a temp dir, points `BIRDCLAW_BIN` at it; the **real** `BirdclawService` spawns it (`--version` probe, `db stats`, `search tweets`) via `execFile` → JSON-parse | Fake CLI logs every argv; check asserts `search tweets local-first …` reached the seam and the parsed tweets carry the searched query |
| `elizacloud.account-status` | `CLOUD_ACCOUNT_STATUS` | `CloudAuthService` authenticates from a seeded key (`isAuthenticated → true`), handler reads `GET /credits/balance` through the typed SDK against a scoped fetch mock; cloud inference/embedding slots pinned off so the plugin never steals the proxy's model handlers | Check asserts the balance endpoint was served and `result.data.balance === 12.5` (the mock) with a top-up URL |
| `meetings.get-transcript` | `GET_MEETING_TRANSCRIPT` | Overrides `MeetingService.dependencyFactory` with a scripted browser adapter + ASR pipeline, drives a **real** `requestJoin` → session state machine → transcript writer to a finalized `"ready"` record (the #15704 finalization path), then reads it back | Check asserts `result.data.transcriptId` matches the finalized session's transcript and the reply carries the transcribed speech |

The `plugin-meetings` scenario is timely given #15704 just added transcript-finalization processing — it exercises that finalize path end-to-end.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)7f:T1ae1,Implements the anchor pipeline from #14806: transcript knowledge fragments are now chunked on **segment boundaries** and carry `startMs`/`endMs`/`segmentIds`/`speakerLabels` time anchors, so a search hit can seek the stored audio and the PII pipeline (#14807) can map a text-PII span in a fragment back to an audio timestamp span.

## What changed

- **`@elizaos/shared/transcripts`** — new `transcriptKnowledgeFragments(segments, maxChars?)`: greedy segment-boundary grouping into ~chunk-sized fragments (default 2000 chars ≈ the documents pipeline's 500-token chunks), never splitting inside a segment; joined fragments reproduce `transcriptPlainText` byte-for-byte. New `TranscriptFragmentInput` type + `TRANSCRIPT_FRAGMENT_MAX_CHARS`.
- **core documents (`packages/core/src/features/documents`)** — new optional `AddDocumentOptions.fragments` seam plumbed through `DocumentService.addDocument` → `processFragmentsSynchronously`. When present, exactly those fragments are embedded + persisted (no token splitter); per-fragment metadata merges onto the stored FRAGMENT memory's jsonb **between** the inherited document metadata and the structural fields, so producers can add anchors but can never corrupt `type`/`documentId`/`position`. Malformed batches **throw before anything is persisted** (empty array, empty text, non-finite/negative/inverted anchors) — no partial writes, no fabricated defaults.
- **producers** — the voice-session mirror (`transcriptKnowledgePayload` → `TranscriptService.mirrorToKnowledge`) and the meetings writer (`MeetingTranscriptWriter.mirrorToKnowledge`) both pass segment-boundary fragments. Fragments inherit `transcriptId`/`audioUrl` from document metadata as before.
- **search projection** — `/api/documents/search` rows now carry `transcriptId`/`startMs`/`endMs` when present (omitted entirely for non-transcript fragments); the core `DOCUMENT` search action prefixes anchored hits with a `[m:ss–m:ss]` clock so agent-facing results cite where in the recording the match sits.

The GC prerequisite (voice-session WAVs invisible to media GC) already landed in #14834. Remaining #14806 scope (UI deep-link seek in the Transcripts player, cloud STT `verbose_json` timestamps, timed-ASR seam for meetings word timings) continues in follow-up PRs on the same issue.

PR #15617 landed the first four fixes (nonce 503 boundary, `validateApiKey` 503, transactional wallet signup, awaited `ensureUserHasApiKey`, postMessage origin) while this lane was in flight; this PR closes the **residual gaps** that survived it, with real-DB proofs.

## Root causes and fixes

### 1. A failing optional welcome grant aborted the whole signup (savepoint containment)
`runWithSignupGrantIpCapDetailed` ran the per-IP count + credit grant **directly on** the wallet-signup transaction (`existingTx`). In Postgres, any failed statement aborts the enclosing transaction — so when the optional bonus grant failed, `grantWalletSignupCredits`'s designed J4 continue-without-bonus catch could never work: the subsequent user insert died with "current transaction is aborted" and the signup failed entirely for an optional bonus.

**Fix:** `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/signup-grant-guard.ts` runs the count + grant in a **SAVEPOINT** (`existingTx.transaction(run)`) when given an enclosing transaction. A failed grant rolls back only the savepoint; signup completes without the bonus. The advisory xact lock is held until the outer transaction commits, which strengthens (never weakens) the per-IP serialization. Proven **red on develop** by the new containment test, green with the fix.

### 2. Unique-violation race recovery missed drizzle-wrapped errors
`isUniqueViolation` in `wallet-signup.ts` checked only the top-level `error.message`. Drizzle raises `DrizzleQueryError` whose message is the failed SQL — the Postgres "duplicate key value violates unique constraint" text lives on `cause`, so a wrapped unique violation was rethrown instead of recovered. Observed concretely in the PGlite runs for this PR.

**Fix:** walk the error `cause` chain and also accept SQLSTATE `23505`.

### 3. Silent config-persist degrade in `eliza auth dev-login` (issue spot 5 — untouched by #15617)
`packages/app-core/src/cli/program/register.auth.ts` logged a **muted aside** when it could not write `ELIZAOS_CLOUD_API_KEY`, then the summary printed the same "not saved (use without --no-save …)" hint as a deliberate `--no-save` — the user believed the agent was cloud-connected while every boot ran without the key.

**Fix (error-policy:J4):** a failed persist is loud — explicit WARNING that the key was **NOT saved**, a copy-pasteable `export ELIZAOS_CLOUD_API_KEY=<key>` remedy, a distinct error-styled summary line, and a first-class `saveError` field on the result (visible to `--json` consumers).

### 4. Real-DB proofs the merged transactional signup lacked
#15617's wallet-signup tests run against a **mocked** `writeTransaction`; nothing proved the actual rollback/no-orphan/idempotent-retry semantics on a real database. New suite `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/__tests__/wallet-signup-orphan-org.test.ts` drives the REAL signup path (real transaction, real credits CTE, real grant guard + advisory lock) against in-process PGlite, with failures injected **in the database** via plpgsql BEFORE INSERT triggers (no mocked seams):
- user-create failure leaves **zero** orgs/users/credit rows (EVM + Solana), and a retry converges with exactly one idempotency-keyed grant;
- pre-fix leaked residue (org + committed grant, no user) is adopted **without double-granting** (balance stays 5, one credit row);
- a second sign-in returns the existing user, creating nothing;
- a failing optional grant is contained: signup completes, `welcomeBonusWithheld: true`, zero credit rows (the savepoint proof).

### 5. Test-coverage gaps in the merged boundary fixes
- `workers-hono-auth.api-key.test.ts`: extended to cover `requireUserOrApiKeyWithOrg` (the guard actually named in the review doc §7b) and the null→**401** invalid-key contrast on both guards — proving the 503 translation didn't swallow the 401 path.
- `auth-default-character-selfheal.test.ts`: proves `ensureUserHasApiKey` is **awaited** (settles on a later tick before session resolution returns — a void-fire would fail the assertion; Workers cancels un-awaited promises).
- `siwe`/`siws` nonce **integration** tests: unlike the merged unit tests (rate limiter stubbed), these run the REAL rate-limit middleware in front of the route and cover the healthy path (nonce issued + binding persisted), the null-client 503, the throwing-Redis 503 (+`Retry-After`), and malformed `chainId` fallback.
